--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed so that the two black nodes (R and P) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths connecting these nodes.

1. **Identify the black nodes**: R and P.
2. **Identify direct neighbors of R and P**:
   - R is connected to B and J.
   - P is connected to I and Q.
3. **List the paths from R to P**:
   - Path 1: R → B → C → H → I → P
   - Path 2: R → B → C → H → Q → P
   - Path 3: R → B → A → N → O → G → H → I → P
   - Path 4: R → B → A → N → O → G → H → Q → P
   - Path 5: R → B → E → S → F → T → Q → P
   - Path 6: R → J → Q → P

4. **Identify the minimal cut set**:
   - To disconnect R and P, we need to remove edges that form part of every path between them.
   - Possible cuts:
     - Remove (R, B) and (R, J) which directly disconnects R from the rest of the graph.
     - Alternatively, removing (J, Q) and (B, C) could form an indirect cut by breaking necessary paths connecting R and P.

At a minimum, removing the edges (R, B) and (R, J) will disconnect R from all paths leading to P. Other combinations may include more edges, making this the minimal cut.

**Output Format:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(R, B), (R, J)]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': [[['J', 'R'], ['Q', 'R']], [['P', 'I'], ['P', 'Q']]]}

   - Neighbors of S: A, B, E
   - Neighbors of R: C, G, O

   - S → A → E → B → T → F → H → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R

   - E → F → B → T → H → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R

   - S → B → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R
   - B → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R

   - E: Connects S to several paths through A and B.
   - B: Connects S directly and to multiple paths through E and T.
   - O: Connects R directly to many paths through Q.
   - G: Connects R directly and to paths through B.

4. **Evaluate the cut-set of nodes to remove the paths:**
   - Removing E disrupts multiple paths: S → A → E → F → H → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R and S → B → E → F → T → H → G → J → I → P → Q → O → R

   - Removing O disrupts the final common paths: → Q → O → R
   - Removing G disrupts paths involving G → J → I → P → Q → O → R

5. **Check the minimum set:**
   To completely disconnect S and R while minimizing the number of nodes removed, the cut-set needs to be chosen such that all paths between S and R are severed. By examining the paths, we identify that removing E, O, and G effectively disconnects all paths.

   - Node I is directly connected to nodes C, H, G, and P.

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - For node A: B, C, and N.
   - For node I: C, H, G, and P.

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - Node B is connected to E, S, R, and J.
   - Node C is connected to N, H, A, and I.
   - Node N is connected to O.
   - Node H is connected to J and Q.
   - Node G is connected to O.
   - Node P is connected to Q and R.

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths from A to I:
     - A → C → I
     - A → B → J → H → I
     - A → B → R → P → I
     - A → B → R → J → H → I

   We need to remove nodes that will disconnect all these paths.

5. **Check possible bottlenecks:**
   - Node B is in three key paths.
   - Node J is in two key paths.
   - Node R is in two key paths.
   - To minimize the number of nodes to remove, we need to find the smallest set of nodes whose removal will break all paths.

   If we remove nodes B and J:
   - The paths A → B → J → H → I and A → B → R → J → H → I are broken.
   - However, the paths A → C → I and A → B → R → P → I are not broken.

   If we remove nodes B and R:
   - The paths A → B → J → H → I and A → B → R → P → I are broken.
   - The paths A → C → I and A → B → R → J → H → I are not broken.

   If we remove nodes B, C, and J:
   - The paths A → C → I, A → B → J → H → I, and A → B → R → J → H → I are broken.
   - The path A → B → R → P → I is not broken.

   If we remove nodes B, C, and R:
   - All paths from A to I are broken.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect nodes A and I is 3, with the cut set being [B, C, R].

--- Model Response ---
To determine the shortest path between the two black nodes, let's first identify the nodes. The black nodes are labeled A and R.

Now, let's trace the paths step-by-step:

1. **Node A connections:**
   - A is directly connected to E and B.

2. **Node B connections:**
   - B is connected to S, E, F, and T.

3. **Following from B:**
   - T is connected to F, which connects to E, and B again.

4. **Node R Connections:**
   - R is directly connected to O and N.

5. **Node O connections:**
   - O is connected to G, and Q.

6. **Node G connections:**
   - G is connected to J, H, C, and N.

7. **Node C connections:**
   - C is connected to G and Q.

8. **Node N connections:**
   - N is connected to C and R.

9. **Finding the shortest path:**
   - One clear path from A to R through shared nodes can be:
     A -> E -> B -> T -> F -> E -> B -> S -> C -> N -> R

Let's check the path:
- A -> E -> B  (3 edges, excluding A)
- B -> S -> C -> N -> R  (4 edges, excluding B)

And another possible path:
- A -> B -> T -> F -> E -> B -> S -> C -> G -> J -> O -> R

So another way remains:

A -> E -> B  (3 edges, excluding A)

Here B connects to S or C

- S -> E -> B -> S -> C
- -> C -> N ->R (4 edges, excluding C)

Clearly, it's not the true shortest:
One of the viable options in theory is using:
           E -> F-> T-> B -> S -> C ->N -> R

And this results in a viable shortest:

- **Node P** is connected to nodes I and Q.
- **Node R** is connected to nodes J and Q.

### Step 2: Trace all paths between P and R
- **Path 1:** P → I → J → R
- **Path 2:** P → Q → R
- **Path 3:** P → Q → J → R

### Step 3: Find the minimum set of edges to remove
- To disconnect the three paths completely:
  - Removing (P, I) breaks Path 1.
  - Removing (P, Q) breaks Paths 2 and 3.
  - Alternatively, removing (Q, R) also breaks Paths 2 and 3.

However, we need to find the minimal set. Removing just (P, Q) or (Q, R) would disconnect P and R through Paths 2 and 3, but not Path 1. Therefore, an additional edge must be removed.

Thus, the minimal set of edges to remove is (P, Q) and either (P, I) or (R, J). We choose (P, I) to ensure all paths are disconnected.

### Final Answer:

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (B and H) in the graph, we need to find the paths that connect these two nodes and then identify the minimal set of edges whose removal will break all such paths.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - Node B
   - Node H

2. **Find immediate neighbors of B:**
   - B is connected to: A, E, F, S, T, J

3. **Find immediate neighbors of H:**
   - H is connected to: E, J, N

4. **Identify paths from B to H through neighboring nodes:**
   - Path through A: No direct connection to H.
   - Path through E: E -> H (Direct)
   - Path through F: No direct connection to H.
   - Path through S: S -> T -> G -> Q -> I -> N -> H (Indirect)
   - Path through T: T -> G -> Q -> I -> N -> H (Direct)
   - Path through J: J -> H (Direct)

5. **Analyze the paths to find bottlenecks:**
   - Direct paths: (B,E), (B,T), (B,J)
   - Indirect paths: B -> S -> T -> G -> Q -> I -> N -> H

6. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing edges (B,E) and (B,J) will disconnect the direct paths.
   - Removing edge (B,T) will break the indirect path through S, T, G, Q, I, N.

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (B and H) is 3. The edges to remove are (B, E), (B, T), and (B, J).

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(B, E), (B, T), (B, J)].
